(iii) show that both
\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime}),

\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
are small terms.

Below we divide this task into several steps.

Step 1: Case
t<\delta^{2}
.

Like in the proof of Lemma
6.23

(see (
6.136
)), we will prove that all the terms in (i)-(iii) above are small individually. We summarize the basic analysis as follows.

(A) [Estimating
\mathcal{R}^{0}
] By adapting the proof of
Lemma
4.12
, it is seen easily that

(6.153)

|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa({\bf W})\Theta(v)E(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

(B) [Estimating
P_{t}W
] As a consequence of Lemma
4.13

(cf. (
4.91
) and (
4.92
)), as well as the estimate on
\mathcal{V}
in Theorem
3.11
,
we have

(6.154)

\displaystyle\big|v_{t}(x)\cdot(P_{t}W(x^{\prime})-P_{t}W(x))\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa({\bf W})\Theta(v)E(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot t^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\kappa({\bf W})\Theta(v)E(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

(C) [Estimating
\mathcal{R}^{0,\delta}
] We start from the expression (
6.146
) and we set
u=x-y
. This yields

(6.155)

\mathcal{R}_{t}^{0,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-x+u\rfloor)-\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor u\rfloor)\right)\\
\times\int_{x}^{x-u}\left(\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)\right)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)duds.

The above expression can be estimated in the usual way as in the continuous
case (see relation (
4.85
)) by writing out
\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}
explicitly
and splitting

\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)=\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)+\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)-\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x).

For simplicity, we only consider the integral corresponding to
\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)
(the other one is in fact easier),
and call this integral
\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
. Specifically, we consider

(6.156)

\mathcal{N}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-x+u\rfloor)-\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor u\rfloor)\right)\\
\times\int_{x}^{x-u}\left(\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)\right)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)duds\,.

With the exact expression of
\hat{p}_{0}(x)=\delta^{-1}\mathds{1}_{(x=0)}
, a simple explicit calculation shows that

(6.157)

\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(u)=-\frac{1}{2\delta^{3}}{\bf 1}_{\{u=0\}}-\frac{1}{16\delta^{3}}{\bf 1}_{\{u=\pm\delta\}}+\frac{1}{8\delta^{3}}{\bf 1}_{\{u=\pm 2\delta\}}+\frac{1}{16\delta^{3}}{\bf 1}_{\{u=\pm 3\delta\}}.

As a result, the
u
-integral in (
6.155
)-(
6.156
)
is supported on finitely many intervals of order
\delta
. Those regions will be paired in order to take advantage of some cancellations due to our expression (
6.157
).

As an example, set
\eta=x^{\prime}-x
and consider the term corresponding to the regions

\lfloor\eta+u\rfloor=0
versus
\lfloor u\rfloor=0
. This yields a term

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\triangleq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\{u:\lfloor\eta+u\rfloor=0\}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor\eta+u\rfloor)-\int_{\{u:\lfloor u\rfloor=0\}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor\eta+u\rfloor)\right)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \times\int_{x}^{x-u}\left(\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)\right)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}duds

(6.158)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2\delta^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{-\eta}^{-\eta+\delta}-\int_{0}^{\delta}\right)\int_{x}^{x-u}\left(\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)\right)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}duds.

We first consider the case when
0<\eta<\delta
. Then (
6.158
) becomes

(6.159)

\mathcal{N}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{2\delta^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{-\eta}^{0}-\int_{-\eta+\delta}^{\delta}\right)\int_{x}^{x-u}\left(\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)-\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(x)\right)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}duds.

In order to estimate this term, we proceed as in Proposition
6.14
and Lemma
4.2
. Namely, we bound the integral with respect to
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
by four terms like in (
4.6
). For notational sake, we will just focus on the fourth term which will be called
\mathcal{N}_{t}^{14,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
. It reads

(6.160)

\mathcal{N}_{t}^{14,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)a^{2\chi}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\\
\times\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{-\eta}^{0}-\int_{-\eta+\delta}^{\delta}\right)e^{-(\lambda+\theta a)s}|t-s|^{-\beta/2}|u|^{\alpha+2\beta}duds\,.

The above integral is handled by elementary methods. Since
0<\eta<\delta
, the quantity
\mathcal{N}_{t}^{14,\delta}
can be upper bounded as

(6.161)

\mathcal{N}_{t}^{14,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\\
\leqslant\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)a^{2\chi}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{[-\eta,0]\cup[-\eta+\delta,\delta]}e^{-(\lambda+\theta a)s}|t-s|^{-\beta/2}|u|^{\alpha+2\beta}duds\,.

Then an explicit computation reveals that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}(t-s)^{-\beta/2}ds\int_{-\eta+\delta}^{\delta}|u|^{\alpha+2\beta}du

(6.162)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}t^{1-\beta/2}\cdot\delta^{\alpha+2\beta}\cdot|\eta|=\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}t^{1-\beta/2}\cdot\delta^{\alpha+2\beta}\cdot|\eta|^{1-2\beta}|\eta|^{2\beta}\leqslant|\eta|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

where we have used the assumptions
t<\delta^{2}
and
|\eta|<\delta

to reach the last inequality. Moreover, the same kind of inequality holds true for the integral on
[-\eta,0]
. Hence plugging relation (
6.162
) into (
6.161
) we get

(6.163)

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{N}_{t}^{14,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}e^{-(\lambda+\theta a)s}a^{2\chi}|\eta|^{2\beta}\delta^{\alpha-\beta}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

where we have used the relation
\chi<\beta/2
,
a\geqslant 1
and where we recall that
\eta=x^{\prime}-x
for the last inequality. We now gather
(
6.161
) with similar estimates coming from the integral with respect to
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
, and we plug those into
(
6.159
). We end up with

(6.164)

\big|\mathcal{N}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

whenever
|x^{\prime}-x|<\delta
.

In order to bound the term
\mathcal{N}^{1,\delta}
in (
6.159
) when
|\eta|=|x-x^{\prime}|\geqslant\delta
, we can directly apply the triangle inequality
to the
u
-integral in (
6.158
) and the resulting estimate
is the same as in (
6.164
). Regions corresponding to other cases in (
6.157
)
lead to the same estimate as well. To conclude, we arrive at the following
estimate:

(6.165)

|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{0,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

(D) We estimate the quantity

(6.166)

\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\triangleq\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})+\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\,,

appearing in (
6.145
), in one go. First observe that we are dealing with the case
t\leqslant\delta^{2}
, for which
\lfloor t\rfloor=0
. Hence the expressions in (
6.147
)-(
6.148
)-(
6.149
) can be reduced to

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{t}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\hat{p}_{\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(\lfloor\xi-y\rfloor)-\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor\xi-y\rfloor)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)dy\big|_{x}^{x^{\prime}}+\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

(6.167)

\displaystyle=\frac{t}{\delta^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\hat{p}_{\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(\lfloor z\rfloor)-\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor z\rfloor)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-z,x^{\prime}-z)\,dz+\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime}).

To proceed further, we shall divide our discussion into two cases:

|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}\geqslant t
or
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t
. The second case will be treated
by a generic argument later on (see Step 2 below). Here we only consider
the first case. Note that integrating against
\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}

or
\hat{p}_{\delta^{2}}^{\delta}
is essentially averaging over
regions of order
\delta.
By applying the triangle inequality to
(
6.167
) in the obvious way and simply using the
\alpha
-Hölder
estimate for
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
, we see that

|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}\cdot t^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha-2\beta}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}.

In addition, recall from (
3.20
) that
\beta>1/3
and
\alpha<1/2
. In particular we have
\alpha<2\beta
. Hence owing to the fact that
|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}\geqslant t
we get

\displaystyle|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}\cdot t^{\beta/2}\cdot t{}^{\alpha/2-\beta}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}

\displaystyle=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}\cdot t{}^{\alpha/2-\beta/2}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

where we have invoked our standing assumption
t<\delta^{2}
for the last inequality. With Proposition
6.14
in mind, we thus obtain the following estimate:

(6.168)

\displaystyle\big|2\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)\cdot\mathcal{R}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})E(a,t)\cdot a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

Summarizing our considerations so far, we report our bounds
(
6.153
), (
6.154
), (
6.165
) and (
6.168
) into (
6.139
) and (
6.141
), we have achieved the following inequality for the case
t\leqslant\delta^{2}
:

(6.169)

\big|\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}^{1,\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}_{{\bf W}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\\
\lesssim\left(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})+\kappa({\bf W})\Theta(v)\right)E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}a^{\chi}(a^{\beta}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

Step 2: Case
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t\wedge\delta^{2\eta}
.
Here we consider a parameter
\eta\in(0,1)

to be chosen later on, and we assume
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t\wedge\delta^{2\eta}
.
In this case, we adopt a generic argument as in Case II of the time-variation
estimate. Under the same notation leading to (
6.136
)
in that part, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}^{1,\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}_{{\bf W}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}^{1,\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}_{{\bf W}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta^{\prime}}{4}}\cdot a^{\chi}(a^{\beta^{\prime}/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta^{\prime}},

where
\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}
is the uniform upper bound on
\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}(w^{\delta})\vee\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}(w)
introduced in (
6.101
).
In view of the constraint (
6.135
) on the parameters,
we can further write

\displaystyle\big|\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}^{1,\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}_{{\bf W}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\cdot a^{\chi}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}

\displaystyle\ \ \ \cdot\left(a^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}+t^{-\beta/2}\cdot t^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{2}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}\right).

Under the current case, we assume
|x^{\prime}-x|\leqslant\delta^{\eta}
. Hence

|x^{\prime}-x|^{2(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}\leqslant\delta^{2\eta(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)},

and since we also assume
t>|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}
we get

t^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{2}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}=t^{-\frac{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}{2}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}\leqslant\delta^{\eta(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

As a result, we arrive at the following estimate whenever
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t\wedge\delta^{2\eta}
:

(6.170)

\big|\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}^{1,\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}_{{\bf W}}^{\mathcal{W}_{t}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\lesssim\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}a^{\chi}(a^{\beta/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\eta(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

Step 3: Case
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
and
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta
.
The following estimate is obtained in the same way leading to the
time-variation estimate in Lemma
6.17
and space-variation estimates in Lemma
6.24
, based on the local central
limit theorem. We only state the final result here. Namely we obtain that

(6.171)

\big|\mathcal{R}_{t}^{0,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}_{t}^{0}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\lesssim E(a,t)\left(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})d_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}}(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)+\Theta(v)\rho(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W})\right)\\
\cdot a^{\chi}(a^{\beta/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\,.

As far as the terms involving
P_{t}
in (
6.145
) are concerned, we first have

(6.172)

\left|\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-v_{t}(x)P_{t}W(x,x^{\prime})\right|\leq\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{B},

where
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are respectively defined by

(6.173)

\mathcal{A}=\big|\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)\big|\cdot\left|\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-P_{t}W(x,x^{\prime})\right|

(6.174)

\mathcal{B}=\big|\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}(x)\big|\cdot\big|P_{t}W(x,x^{\prime})\big|.

According to Lemma
4.13
, the quantity
\mathcal{B}
is bounded as

(6.175)

\mathcal{B}\lesssim E(a,t)d_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}}(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)\cdot\kappa({\bf W})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}.

To estimate
\mathcal{A}
, we further write

\displaystyle\left|\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-P_{t}W(x,x^{\prime})\right|

(6.176)

\displaystyle\leqslant\big|\big(\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}-P_{t}\big)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|+\big|P_{t}\big(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}-W\big)(x,x^{\prime})\big|=:\mathcal{A}_{1}+\mathcal{A}_{2}.

As in Lemma
4.13
, the
\mathcal{A}_{2}
-term is easily
estimated as

(6.177)

\big|P_{t}\big(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}-W\big)(x,x^{\prime})\big|\lesssim\rho\big(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}\big)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}.

To estimate the
\mathcal{A}_{1}
-term, for simplicity we only consider
the case when
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}>t
(the other case is treated in the same
way as Case (i) in the proof of Lemma
4.13
). Now for
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}>t
,
we write

(6.178)

\big(\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}-P_{t}\big)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)-p_{t}(y)\right)\big(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-y)\big)dy.

Recall from the local CLT (cf. Theorem
2.25
) and the uniform
Gaussian estimates (cf. Proposition
2.26
) for the
heat kernels that

\big|\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)-p_{t}(y)\big|\lesssim\frac{\delta^{2}}{t^{3/2}},\quad\text{ and}\quad\big|\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)\big|\vee\big|p_{t}(y)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-Cy^{2}/t}.

Plugging those inequalities into (
6.178
), it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{R}}\big|\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)-p_{t}(y)\big|^{1/2}\cdot\big|\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)-p_{t}(y)\big|^{1/2}\cdot\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-y)\big|dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta}{t^{3/4}}\cdot t^{1/4}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-Cy^{2}/2t}\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-y)\big|dy

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-Cw^{2}/2}\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-\sqrt{t}w)\big|dw

(6.179)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}\cdot a^{\chi}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\,|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}.

Similar to the analysis leading to (
4.92
),
we write

|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}=t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot t^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha-2\beta}.

Noting that
\alpha-2\beta<0
and
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}>t
in the current
case, simple algebra shows that

t^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha-2\beta}<t^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad t^{-\beta/2}|x-x^{\prime}|^{2\beta}<t^{\beta/2}\leq T^{\beta/2}.

Therefore we get

|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}\leq C_{T}\,t^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}.

Plugging this inequality in (
6.179
) we obtain

(6.180)

\mathcal{A}_{1}\lesssim a^{\chi}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\cdot\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}t^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{2}}=a^{\chi}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}\cdot\left(\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}\right)^{1-(\alpha-\beta)}\leqslant a^{\chi}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

where the last inequality holds since
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
in the
current scenario. To summarise, we report (
6.180
) and (
6.177
) into (
6.176
). This yields

\mathcal{A}\lesssim\Theta(v)\left(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\delta^{\alpha-\beta}+\rho\big(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}\big)\right)\cdot E(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}.

Together with (
6.175
) and recalling (
6.172
), we thus arrive at the following estimate:

(6.181)

\displaystyle\big|\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)\hat{P}_{t}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-v_{t}(x)P_{t}W(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\Theta(v)\left(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\delta^{\alpha-\beta}+\rho(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W})\right)+\kappa({\bf W})d_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}}(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)\right]\cdot E(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}.

Now it remains to estimate the quantities
\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime}),

\mathcal{K}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
defined by (
6.148
),
(
6.149
) respectively. We will divide those estimates in two substeps.

Step 3-1: estimate for
\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}
.
Our claim here is that whenever
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
and
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta
we have:

(6.182)

|\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

In order to prove (
6.182
), we return to the expression of
\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
in
terms of
\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}
. Namely invoking the discrete heat equation (
2.78
) in (
6.148
) we get

(6.183)

\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)\sigma^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor\xi-y\rfloor)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)dy\big|_{x}^{x^{\prime}}.

We now split the discussion in two cases.

Case A
:
|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}\geqslant t.
In this case, set
y=\xi-\sqrt{t}w
in (
6.183
). We obtain

\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\frac{(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)\sigma^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\left(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x-\sqrt{t}w)\right)\sqrt{t}dw.

We resort to the fact that
t-\lfloor t\rfloor\leqslant\delta^{2}
and to the Hölder continuity of
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
. Taking into account the upper bound (
2.80
), which reads

\big|\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{3/2}}e^{-Cw^{2}}\quad\text{ for all }\quad t\geqslant\delta^{2},w\in\mathbb{R},

we end up with

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}\cdot\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\cdot t^{-1}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\cdot t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}\cdot t^{\beta/2-1}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha-2\beta}.

Moreover, since we assume
|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}\geqslant t
and we have

\alpha<2\beta
, we discover that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\cdot t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}\cdot t^{\beta/2-1}t{}^{\alpha/2-\beta}

\displaystyle=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\cdot t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}\cdot t{}^{\alpha/2-\beta/2-1}.

Eventually recall that
t>\delta^{2}
in this step, and
\alpha-\beta-2<0
. This yields

(6.184)

\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

which is compatible with our claim (
6.143
).

Case B:

|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t
. Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(y)dy=0
,
we can insert this quantity in the expression (
6.183
) for

\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
. This yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)\sigma^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor)-\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\right)\left(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\right)dy

(6.185)

\displaystyle=\frac{(t-\lfloor t\rfloor)\sigma^{2}}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{\lfloor x-y\rfloor}^{\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor}\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor z\rfloor)\left(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\right)dzdy,

where the discrete gradient
\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}
is given by (
2.76
):

(\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}f)(x)\triangleq\frac{(\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}f)(x+\delta)-(\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}f)(x)}{\delta}.

Moreover, the third order difference
\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor z\rfloor)

also satisfies the uniform Gaussian estimate in Proposition
2.26
. Namely we have

(6.186)

\big|\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor z\rfloor)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{t^{2}}e^{-Cz^{2}/t}\quad\text{ for all }\quad t\geqslant\delta^{2},\ z\in\mathbb{R}.

Suppose that
\lfloor x-y\rfloor
and
\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor
have
the same sign, say

\lfloor x-y\rfloor\geqslant\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor\geqslant 0.

Since
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta
under the current discussion, it is
easily seen that

(6.187)

\int_{\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor}^{\lfloor x-y\rfloor}e^{-Cz^{2}/t}dz\lesssim|x^{\prime}-x|e^{-C(x^{\prime}-y)^{2}/t}.

Plugging (
6.186
) and (
6.187
) into (
6.185
), we obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}t^{-2}|x^{\prime}-x|\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-C(x^{\prime}-y)^{2}/t}\cdot\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\big|dy

(6.188)

\displaystyle=\delta^{2}t^{-3/2}|x^{\prime}-x|\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-Cw^{2}}\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\big|dw,

where we have set
y=x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w
for the second identity. Next, owing to the Hölder regularity of
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
and invoking
|x^{\prime}-x|\leqslant\sqrt{t}
again (also noting
x,x^{\prime}\in[-a,a]
and
a\geqslant 1
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})(|x^{\prime}|+|x|+\sqrt{t}|w|)^{\chi}\cdot(|x^{\prime}-x|+\sqrt{t}|w|)^{\alpha}

\displaystyle\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})(2a+\sqrt{T}|w|)^{\chi}\cdot(\sqrt{t}+\sqrt{t}|w|)^{\alpha}

(6.189)

\displaystyle\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{\alpha/2}(2+2\sqrt{T}|w|)^{\chi}(1+|w|)^{\alpha}.

Reporting this inequality into (
6.188
), we can write

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\cdot\delta^{2}t^{\alpha/2-3/2}|x^{\prime}-x|

\displaystyle=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}t^{\alpha/2+\beta/2-3/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{1-2\beta}.

Now recall once more that
|x^{\prime}-x|<\sqrt{t}
and
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
. We thus obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}t^{\alpha/2-\beta/2-1}

(6.190)

\displaystyle\leqslant\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

This fits our claim (
6.171
) when
\lfloor x-y\rfloor
and
\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor
have the same sign.
If
\lfloor x-y\rfloor
and
\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor
have different
signs, say

x-y\leqslant 0\leqslant x^{\prime}-y\iff x\leqslant y\leqslant x^{\prime},

then we simply bound (owing to (
6.186
)) the quantity

\tilde{\nabla}^{3,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t\rfloor}^{\delta}
by
t^{-2}
. Plugging this crude estimate in (
6.185
) we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}t^{-2}|x^{\prime}-x|\cdot\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\big|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x)\big|dy

\displaystyle\leqslant\delta^{2}t^{-2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}\cdot\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2}t^{-2+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2+\alpha-2\beta}.

Thanks to the assumption
|x^{\prime}-x|<\sqrt{t}
and
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
, we get

(6.191)

\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

Gathering (
6.190
), (
6.191
) and (
6.184
), we have thus obtained an inequality for
\mathcal{Q}_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
which is compatible with (
6.171
).

Step 3-2: Estimate for
\mathcal{K}^{\delta}
.

Let us focus our attention on the term
\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
defined by (
6.149
). Here
under the current case, if
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\sqrt{t},
we assert that

(6.192)

|\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta}.

If
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta^{\tau}
where
\tau\in(0,1)
is a given
number to be chosen later on, we will see that

(6.193)

|\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-2\beta\tau}.

The proof of those claims goes as follows: by the definition of
\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)\left(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}-y)-\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x-y)\right)dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\int_{0}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}-y)dy-\int_{0}^{\delta}\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x-y)dy\right).

As a result, we have

|\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\leqslant\frac{1}{\delta}\int_{0}^{\delta}\left(|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x^{\prime},x^{\prime}-y)|+|\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x-y)|\right)dy.

By using the standard
\alpha
-Hölder estimate for
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
,
we obtain that

|\mathcal{K}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})|\lesssim\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})a^{\chi}\delta^{\alpha}.

If
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\sqrt{t}
, since
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
we can
further write

\delta^{\alpha}=\delta^{\alpha}|x^{\prime}-x|^{-2\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\leqslant\delta^{\alpha}t^{-\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\leqslant t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-\beta},

which leads to (
6.192
). If
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta^{\tau},

we have

\delta^{\alpha}=\delta^{\alpha-2\beta\tau}\delta^{2\beta\tau}t^{\beta/2}t^{-\beta/2}\leqslant C_{T}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}t^{-\beta/2}\cdot\delta^{\alpha-2\beta\tau},

which leads to (
6.193
).

Step 4: Conclusion.

We first remark that Steps 1, 2 and 3 above cover all possibilities. Indeed,
the complement of Steps 1, 3 is the case when
t\geqslant\delta^{2}

and
|x^{\prime}-x|<\delta
. But this situation is contained in Step 2:

t\geqslant\delta^{2},\ |x^{\prime}-x|<\delta\implies|x^{\prime}-x|^{2}<t\wedge\delta^{2\tau}

since
\tau\in(0,1)
. In view of the estimates (
6.170
)
and (
6.193
), we now choose
\tau
to be such that

\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)=\alpha-2\beta\tau\iff\tau=\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}.

The resulting rate is
\delta^{r}
with
r=\frac{\alpha}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)
.
Combining all the ingredients obtained so far, the desired estimate
(
6.143
) follows.
∎

6.5.
Developing Step 2: comparing
\mathcal{J}^{\delta}
and
\eta

In Section
6.1
we mentioned that the convergence of
\tilde{v}^{\delta}
to
v
also relied on the convergence of a series of deterministic type terms. Specifically, consider the path
\eta
defined by (
6.6
). This path is approximated by the discrete process
\mathcal{J}_{t}^{\delta}
introduced in (
6.12
). The second step of the strategy recalled in Section
6.1
leads to inequality
(
6.18
). We will detail this step now.
Our estimate on the controlled distance between
\mathcal{J}^{\delta}

and
\eta
is summarized in the proposition below.

Proposition 6.28
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\chi,\theta
be exponents satisfying condition (
3.20
). We consider fixed coefficients
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(\beta,\alpha)
. The upper bound
\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}
on the rough path norms of
\mathcal{W}^{1,\delta}
and
\mathcal{W}
is given by (
6.101
). Recall that
\mathcal{J}^{\delta}
is introduced in (
6.12
),
while
\eta
is the path given by (
6.6
). Then we have

(6.194)

d_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}}(\mathcal{J}^{\delta},\eta)\leqslant C\left((\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}})\delta^{\beta}+(1+\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}))\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}(\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}}+\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}\delta^{\alpha-2\chi})\right).

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Proposition

6.28
, for which we now outline a strategy.
In view of the definition of
d_{\hat{U}^{\delta},{\bf W}}(\mathcal{J}^{\delta},\eta)
,
the comparison between
\mathcal{J}^{\delta}
and
\eta
boils down
to estimating four types of differences: uniform, time-variation,
space-variation and remainder. Since a substantial part of the analysis
here is a technical repetition of the previous section (indeed it
is simpler than Step 3 except for the estimation of one term which
we will point out later on), for most of the time we will only consider
uniform distance estimates. In addition, we will only consider
\mathcal{J}_{t}^{\delta}(x)-\eta_{t}(x)

on grid points
(t,x)\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
.
The adaptation to non-grid points is easy since
\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}(x)

is defined through piecewise linear interpolation (cf. Definition
6.2
).

6.5.1.
A decomposition of
\mathcal{J}_{t}^{\delta}(x)

Recall that in (
6.12
), the quantity
\mathcal{J}_{t}^{\delta}
was implicit. As a starting point, we shall first compute
\mathcal{J}_{t}^{\delta}(x)

explicitly. This is the content of the lemma below.

Lemma 6.29
.

Recall that
v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)

is the discrete process defined by (
6.1
), in which
\eta_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)

and
\mathcal{I}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x,y)
are given by (
6.2
) and (
6.4
)
respectively. For each
(t_{k},x)\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
,
we have

(6.195)

\mathcal{J}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)+\eta_{t_{k+1}}^{\delta}(x)\right)+\frac{1}{2}\left(v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t_{k+1}}^{\delta}(x)\right)+\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x).

The function
\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)
in the above equation
is an error term defined by

\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{1,\delta}(x)+\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{2,\delta}(x),

where
\mathcal{E}^{1,\delta}
and
\mathcal{E}^{2,\delta}
are respectively given by

(6.196)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{1,\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4}\delta^{3}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-z\rfloor)\mathcal{I}_{0}^{\delta}(x,z)

(6.197)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{2,\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\left(\int_{y}^{\lfloor y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t_{k}-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(z)\right)dyds.

Proof.

Using the definition (
6.12
) of
\mathcal{J}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)
,
we first write

(6.198)

\mathcal{J}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=\tilde{v}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t_{k}-s\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\left(\int_{x}^{\lfloor y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(z)\right)dyds+\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{2,\delta},

where
\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{2,\delta}(x)
is defined by (
6.197
).
Next, due to the fact that both
t_{k}
and
x
are on the grid
\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
, the integral on the right hand side of
(
6.198
) can further be expressed
as

(6.199)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t_{k}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}-s}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\left(\int_{x}^{\lfloor y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(z)\right)dyds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor t_{k}-t_{j}\rfloor}^{\delta}(x-z)\left(\int_{x}^{z}\tilde{v}_{s}^{\delta}(w)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(w)\right)ds.

Since
s\mapsto\tilde{v}_{s}^{\delta}(z)
is linear on
[t_{j-1},t_{j}]

by definition, it is easily seen that

\int_{t_{j-1}}^{t_{j}}\tilde{v}_{s}^{\delta}(w)ds=\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\left(\tilde{v}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(w)+\tilde{v}_{t_{j}}^{\delta}(w)\right).

As a result, the term
\mathcal{G}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}
in (
6.199
) satisfies

(6.200)

\mathcal{G}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=\frac{\delta}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}-t_{j}}^{\delta}(x-z)\int_{x}^{z}\frac{\delta^{2}}{2}\left(\tilde{v}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(w)+\tilde{v}_{t_{j}}^{\delta}(w)\right)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(w).

By using the piecewise linear construction of
\tilde{v}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(w)

and
\hat{U}^{\delta}(w)
, given respectively in (
6.9
) and (
5.2
), it is easily checked that

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{z}\tilde{v}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(w)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(w)

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in\llbracket x+\delta,z\rrbracket}\int_{u-\delta}^{u}\frac{(u-w)v_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(u-\delta)+(w-u+\delta)v_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(u)}{\delta}\times\frac{\bar{U}^{\delta}(u)}{\delta}dw

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\llbracket x+\delta,z\rrbracket\end{subarray}}\frac{v_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(u-\delta)+v_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(u)}{2}\,\bar{U}^{\delta}(u)=\mathcal{I}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(x,z).

where the last equality is a direct consequence of (
6.4
).
By substituting this into the expression (
6.200
), we
obtain that

\mathcal{G}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=\frac{\delta^{3}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}-t_{j}}^{\delta}(x-z)\times\frac{\mathcal{I}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(x,z)+\mathcal{I}_{t_{j}}^{\delta}(x,z)}{2}.

Hence going back to (
6.199
) and (
6.198
), we end up with

(6.201)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)

\displaystyle=v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)+\frac{\delta^{3}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}-t_{j}}^{\delta}(x-z)\times\frac{\mathcal{I}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(x,z)+\mathcal{I}_{t_{j}}^{\delta}(x,z)}{2}+\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{2,\delta}(x),

where we note that
\tilde{v}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)=v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)
since

(t_{k},x)
is assumed to be a grid point.

On the other hand, by using the discrete equation (
6.1
) for
v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)
,
it is seen that

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{3}}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{z\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{t_{k}-t_{j}}^{\delta}(x-z)\times\frac{\mathcal{I}_{t_{j-1}}^{\delta}(x,z)+\mathcal{I}_{t_{j}}^{\delta}(x,z)}{2}

(6.202)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)+\eta_{t_{k+1}}^{\delta}(x)\right)-\frac{1}{2}\left(v_{t_{k}}^{\delta}(x)+v_{t_{k+1}}^{\delta}(x)\right)+\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{1,\delta}(x),

where
\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{1,\delta}(x)
is defined by (
6.196
).
By substituting (
6.202
) into (
6.201
), the
desired decomposition (
6.195
) thus follows.
∎

In view of Lemma
6.29
, in order to compare
\mathcal{J}^{\delta}

with
\eta,
it is clear that there are three main ingredients to
be developed:

(i) compare
\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta_{t}^{\delta}(x)+\eta_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)\right)

with
\eta_{t}(x)
;

(ii) show that
v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)
is small;

(iii) show that the error term
\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\delta}(x)
is small.

We now implement these three steps separately.

6.5.2.
Comparing
\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta_{t}^{\delta}(x)+\eta_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)\right)

with
\eta_{t}(x)

We begin our comparison procedure by looking at the difference between
\frac{1}{2}\left(\eta_{t}^{\delta}(x)+\eta_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)\right)
and
\eta_{t}(x)
. Notice that the analysis of this term is
easy, since it only involves the input functions
f_{0}(x),g_{t}(x)

as well as the heat kernels. The resulting estimate is summarised
in the lemma below.

Lemma 6.30
.

Let
f_{0},g\in\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}
be given functions with some

L>0
. Recall that
\eta
and
\eta^{\delta}
are respectively defined by (
6.6
) and (
6.2
). The distance
d_{\hat{U}^{\delta},{\bf W}}
is introduced in (
6.11
). Then we have

(6.203)

d_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta},{\bf W}}\left(\frac{1}{2}(\eta_{\cdot}^{\delta}(\cdot)+\eta_{\cdot+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(\cdot)),\eta\right)\leqslant C\cdot\left(\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}\right)\cdot\delta^{\beta}.

Proof.

For simplicity, we only discuss the remainder estimate for the
f_{0}
-part.
Estimates of all other parts are routine repetitions of the same kind
of analysis. More specifically, let us define

\eta_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)\triangleq\delta\sum_{y\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{1,\delta}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(x-y)f_{0}(y),\quad\text{ and }\quad\eta_{t}^{1}(x)\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}p_{t}(x-y)f_{0}(y)dy

respectively. We want to estimate the difference
\eta_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-\eta_{t}^{1}(x,x^{\prime})

where
t\in(0,T]\cap\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}
and
x,x^{\prime}\in[-a,a]\cap\delta\mathbb{Z}
. For this purpose, we first resort to a discrete integration by parts like in Proposition
2.24
. Hence
\eta^{1,\delta}
can be recast as

(6.204)

\eta_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)=\delta\sum_{y\in\delta\mathbb{Z}}\nabla_{x}^{1,\delta}f_{0}(x-y)\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(y)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)dy.

In the same way, due to our regularity assumption on
f
, a simple integration by parts yields

(6.205)

\eta_{t}^{1}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\partial_{x}f_{0}(x-y)p_{t}(y)dy.

We now express the increments
\eta^{1,\delta}_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
, Thanks to
(
6.204
) and a change of variable
y=\sqrt{t}w
, we get

(6.206)

\eta_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)\left(\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-y\rfloor)-\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\right)dy\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{t}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\left(\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\right)dy

Similarly, by using (
6.205
) we also have

(6.207)

\eta_{t}^{1}(x,x^{\prime})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{t}(y)\left(\partial_{x}f_{0}(x^{\prime}-y)-\partial_{x}f_{0}(x-y)\right)dy\\
=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{t}(y)\left(\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-y)dz\right)dy=\int_{\mathbb{R}}p_{1}(w)\left(\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-\sqrt{t}w)dz\right)dw.

Putting together (
6.206
) and (
6.207
), we thus obtain the identity

\eta_{t}^{1,\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-\eta_{t}^{1}(x,x^{\prime})=A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})+B_{t}(x,x^{\prime}),

where
A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
and
B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
are respectively defined by

(6.208)

\displaystyle A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\left(\sqrt{t}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-p_{1}(w)\right)\left(\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-\sqrt{t}w)dz\right)dw,

(6.209)

\displaystyle B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})

\displaystyle\triangleq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{t}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\,M_{x,x^{\prime}}(\sqrt{t}w)dw,

where for
a\in\mathbb{R}
we define

M_{x,x^{\prime}}(a)=\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-a\rfloor)-\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-a\rfloor)-\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-a)dz.

We will now estimate the terms
A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
and
B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
.
Let us start by estimating the term
A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
introduced in (
6.208
). In order to bound this term, we proceed as in Section
6.4.1
. Namely we write
\hat{p}^{\delta}
in terms of the discrete kernel

p^{d}
and we invoke Theorem
2.25
. We let the patient reader check that

\left|\sqrt{t}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-p_{1}(w)\right|\leqslant C_{1}\cdot\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}e^{-C_{2}w^{2}},

for all
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
and
w\in\mathbb{R}.
Plugging this inequality into (
6.208
), it follows
that

\big|A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C_{1}\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-C_{2}w^{2}}\left(\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\big|\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-\sqrt{t}w)\big|dz\right)dw.

In addition, the right hand side above can be bounded invoking the fact that
f\in{\mathcal{C}}^{3}_{L}
and Definition
6.4
we obtain

\big|A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C_{3}\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{t}}\cdot|x-x^{\prime}|\cdot a^{L}\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}.

Whenever
t\geqslant\delta^{2}
and
x\in[-a,a]
we thus get

\displaystyle\big|A_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{3}t^{-\beta/2}\delta^{\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot a^{L+1-2\beta}\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}

(6.210)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{4}\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}E(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta},

where we have absorbed some power of
a
into
E(a,t)
term for the last step.

Next, we estimate
B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
in (
6.209
). By the definition of
\nabla_{x}^{1,\delta}
,
it is easily seen that

\displaystyle\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime}+\delta-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-f_{0}(x+\delta-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)}{\delta}-\frac{f_{0}(x^{\prime}-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-f_{0}(x-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)}{\delta}

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\frac{\partial_{x}f_{0}(z+\delta-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\partial_{x}f_{0}(z-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)}{\delta}dz

\displaystyle=\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z+\theta\delta-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)d\theta\right)dz.

Using again Definition
6.4
and our assumption
f_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{3}_{L}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Big|M_{x,x^{\prime}}(\sqrt{t}w)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x^{\prime}-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\nabla^{1,\delta}f_{0}(\lfloor x-\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-\sqrt{t}w)dz\Big|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{x}^{x^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z+\theta\delta-\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)-\partial_{xx}^{2}f_{0}(z-\sqrt{t}w)\right)d\theta dz\right|

(6.211)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}a^{L}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|\cdot\delta\cdot(1+|w|)^{L}.

In addition, recall from (
6.61
) that

(6.212)

\big|\sqrt{t}\hat{p}_{t}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{t}w\rfloor)\big|\leqslant Ce^{-C_{2}w^{2}}.

By substituting (
6.211
) and (
6.212
) into the
expression (
6.209
) of
B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})
, we can bound it as

\big|B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}a^{L}|x^{\prime}-x|\cdot\delta.

Now one can perform the same kind of manipulation as for (
6.210
) in order to get

\displaystyle\big|B_{t}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}a^{L+1-2\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta

(6.213)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}E(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta.

By putting (
6.210
) and (
6.213
) together, we
obtain the remainder estimate for
\eta^{1,\delta}-\eta^{1}
, which
contributes to one term in the rough path distance. All other terms
(uniform distance, spatial and time variations) and also the
g(t,x)
-part
are dealt with in a similar way. The resulting estimate is given by
(
6.203
).
∎

6.5.3.
Estimating
v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)

This part requires some care since we do not view
t\mapsto v_{t}^{\delta}(\cdot)

as a Hölder-continuous path taking values in spatial rough paths.
As opposed to that, we shall consider the path
G_{t}^{\delta}=v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)
as an element of the (discrete) space
\mathcal{B}^{\theta,\lambda}
which is introduced in Definition
3.9
, and we will prove that the norm of
G^{\delta}
is small. Our estimate is summarized in the following lemma.

Lemma 6.31
.

In the decomposition (
6.195
), let
G^{\delta}
be the path given by

(6.214)

G^{\delta}_{t}(x)\equiv v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x),

defined for
(t,x)\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
. We consider
G
as a discrete path controlled by
\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}
, with a null derivative. Pick
\alpha,\beta,\chi
satisfying (
3.20
), as well as
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(\beta,\alpha)
, so that for all
a\geqslant 1
and

t\in[0,T]
we have

(6.215)

E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\beta^{\prime}/2}\leqslant E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2},

where we recall that
E^{\theta}(a,t)=e^{\lambda t+\theta a+\theta at}
.
As usual we choose
\gamma=(\alpha-\beta)/4
and
\lambda
is such that
(
6.8
) is fulfilled. The controlled norms
\Theta
are introduced in (
3.27
). Then we have

(6.216)

\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\lambda}(G^{\delta})\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}(1+\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}))\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\cdot\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}}.

Proof.

As usual, based on the definition of our rough path metric, we need
to develop four types of estimates. We again use the trick of adjusting
exponents based on the uniform boundedness of
v_{t}^{\delta}(x)

proved in Proposition
6.14
.

Step 1: uniform estimate.
Let
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(\beta,\alpha)

be fixed parameters such that (
6.215
) is verified. In particular for all
a\geqslant 1
and
t\in[0,T]
we have

E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\beta^{\prime}/2}\leqslant E^{\theta}(a,t)\ \ \ \forall a\geqslant 1,t\in[0,T].

It follows from Proposition
6.14
that

(6.217)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\beta^{\prime}/2}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}\leqslant\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)\delta^{\beta^{\prime}},

where
\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}
denotes a uniform upper bound of

\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\lambda}(v^{\delta})
. This gives the uniform estimate
in the definition of the rough path norm of
(t,x)\mapsto G_{t}^{\delta}(x).

Step 2: time variation estimate
. We simply decompose the time variation as follows for
s,t\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\delta\mathbb{Z}
:

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x)-G_{s}^{\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant\big|v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)\big|+\big|v_{s+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{s}^{\delta}(x)\big|.

Next we recall that
(\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime})
are chosen as in (
6.215
). Invoking the
\delta
-uniform bound on
v^{\delta}
obtained in Proposition
6.14
, we get

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x)-G_{s}^{\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant 2\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}=2\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}\delta^{\beta}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

Since
s,t
on the grid
\delta^{2}\mathbb{N},
satisfy
t-s\geqslant\delta^{2}
, we discover that

(6.218)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x)-G_{s}^{\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant 2\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}|t-s|^{\beta/2}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

Step 3: the space variation estimate
. This term is handled
exactly like the time increment in Step 2. We let the reader check that for all

x,x^{\prime}\in[-a,a]
and
t\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}
we have

(6.219)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant 2\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

Step 4: remainder term and conclusion.
Since our term ansatz stipulates that the derivative
\partial_{\hat{U}^{\delta}}
vanishes,
we are now left with an estimate of the remainder for
G^{\delta}
. This is
achieved by elaborating on the upper bound (
6.219
) in order to get a second order estimate in
x^{\prime}-x
. This step is detailed in
Lemma
6.32
below for the sake of clarity. Gathering this lemma and (
6.217
)-(
6.218
)-(
6.219
), this finishes the proof of our claim (
6.216
).
∎

We now state and prove the announced estimate of the remainder for the controlled process
G^{\delta}
defined above.

Lemma 6.32
.

Under the same conditions as for Lemma
6.22
, let

t\in\llparenthesis 0,T\rrbracket
and
x,x^{\prime}\in\llbracket-a,a\rrbracket
. Also recall that the path
G^{\delta}
is defined by (
6.214
). Then we have

(6.220)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}(1+\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}))E^{\theta}(a,t)\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}a^{\chi}(a^{\beta/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}}.

Proof.

We divide the discussion into three (not necessarily disjoint) cases.
Note that such type of consideration already appears in the proof
of Lemma
6.25
. In the sequel
\tau
is a parameter in
[0,1]
which will be chosen appropriately.

Case I:
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\delta^{\tau}
.

In this case we decompose

G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})
as

(6.221)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x)\big|+\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x^{\prime})\big|.

Then for
x,x^{\prime}\in\llbracket-a,a\rrbracket
one can apply the uniform estimate
(
6.217
) and obtain

(6.222)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}.

One can then conclude by invoking the fact that
\delta^{\tau}\leqslant|x^{\prime}-x|
. That is we get

\displaystyle\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}\delta^{2\tau\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-2\tau\beta}

(6.223)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-2\tau\beta}.

Case II:
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\sqrt{t}
.
We choose
\beta^{\prime},\theta^{\prime}

such that (
6.215
) is verified. Then we start our estimation procedure like in (
6.221
), which yields inequality
(
6.222
). Now one can decompose the right hand side of
(
6.222
) in order to get

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{-2\beta}t^{\beta/2}.

Next we resort to the fact that
|x^{\prime}-x|\geqslant\sqrt{t}
to write

\displaystyle\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}t^{-\beta/2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta}t^{-\beta/2}.

Eventually, thanks to the relation
t\geq\delta^{2}
we obtain a relation which is similar to (
6.223
):

(6.224)

\big|G_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

Case III:
|x^{\prime}-x|\leqslant\sqrt{t}\wedge\delta^{\tau}
.
Recall that in (
6.142
) we have written a decomposition for the process
\tilde{v}_{t}^{\delta}
. Specialized on the grid
\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
this can be read as

(6.225)

v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-v_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})=-2\left(v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})+\mathcal{R}^{v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime}),

where we have written
\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}
instead

\mathcal{R}_{\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}
in order to alleviate our notation. We now bound the two terms in the right hand side of (
6.225
).

In order to bound the derivative term in the right hand side of (
6.225
), consider again
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
and
\beta^{\prime}\in(\beta,\alpha)
. Then invoking Proposition
6.14
and the fact that
\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})
is
uniformly bounded in
\delta
, we get

(6.226)

\big|\left(v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta^{\prime}/2}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}.

Recall that we are now dealing with the case
|x-x^{\prime}|\leqslant\delta^{\tau}
and
x,x^{\prime}\in\llbracket-a,a\rrbracket
.
However, we also have
|x^{\prime}-x|\geq\delta
, due to the fact that both
x
and
x^{\prime}
belong to the grid
\delta\mathbb{Z}
.
Therefore one is enabled to write

\delta^{\beta^{\prime}}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha}=\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\left(\frac{\delta}{|x^{\prime}-x|}\right)^{\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{\alpha-\beta}\leqslant\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}a^{\alpha-\beta}\,.

Reporting this inequality in the right hand side of (
6.226
) yields

(6.227)

\big|\left(v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta^{\prime}/2+\alpha-\beta}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

In addition, we have assumed that (
6.215
) is verified. Hence we obtain

E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta^{\prime}/2+\alpha-\beta}\leqslant CE^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta/2}.

Plugging this information into (
6.227
), it follows that

(6.228)

\big|\left(v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x)-v_{t}^{\delta}(x)\right)\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi+\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}.

We now turn to an estimate of the remainder terms in (
6.225
). We shall bound each term in the difference individually,
namely write

\big|\mathcal{R}^{v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant\big|\mathcal{R}^{v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|+\big|\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\,.

Then we resort to Proposition
6.14
and the definition of
\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\lambda}
in (
3.27
) in order to obtain

(6.229)

\big|\mathcal{R}^{v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)\lambda^{\gamma^{\prime}}a^{\chi}(a^{\beta^{\prime}/2}+t^{-\beta^{\prime}/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta^{\prime}}.

where
\gamma^{\prime}\triangleq\frac{\alpha-\beta^{\prime}}{4}.
Since
|x^{\prime}-x|\leqslant\delta^{\tau},

we have

|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta^{\prime}}=|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta^{\prime}-2\beta}\leqslant|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{2\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

In addition, since
|x^{\prime}-x|\leqslant\sqrt{t},
we also have

\displaystyle t^{-\beta^{\prime}/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot t^{-(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}

\displaystyle\leqslant t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot|x^{\prime}-x|^{\beta^{\prime}-\beta}\leqslant t^{-\beta/2}|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

Reporting those values into (
6.229
) and recalling that (
6.215
) holds true we end up with

(6.230)

\big|\mathcal{R}^{v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})-\mathcal{R}^{v_{t}^{\delta}}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\lambda^{\gamma^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}(a^{\beta/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

Hence gathering (
6.228
) and (
6.230
) into (
6.225
), we have obtained

(6.231)

\big|v_{t+\delta^{2}}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})-v_{t}^{\delta}(x,x^{\prime})\big|\leqslant C\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\lambda^{\gamma^{\prime}}E^{\theta}(a,t)a^{\chi}(a^{\beta/2}+t^{-\beta/2})|x^{\prime}-x|^{2\beta}\cdot\delta^{\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}.

Combining cases I to III, it is not hard to see that the optimal
choice of
\tau
is such that

\beta^{\prime}-2\tau\beta=\tau(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)\iff\tau=\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}.

The resulting
\delta
-factors is then found to be
\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}}
.
This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

6.5.4.
Estimating
\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\delta}(x)

The last term we have to estimate in order to quantify the convergence of

\mathcal{J}^{\delta}
in (
6.194
) is
\mathcal{E}_{t_{k}}^{\delta}
. We label another lemma in that direction

Lemma 6.33
.

In the decomposition (
6.195
), consider the term

(6.232)

\mathcal{E}_{t}^{\delta}(x)=\mathcal{E}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)+\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x),

which is defined for
(t,x)\in\delta^{2}\mathbb{N}\times\delta\mathbb{Z}
and where
\mathcal{E}_{t}^{1,\delta},\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2,\delta}
are introduced in (
6.196
)- (
6.197
). We pick
\alpha,\beta,\chi
satisfying (
3.20
) as well as
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
such that

(6.233)

E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)a^{2\chi+\beta/2}\leqslant E^{\theta}(a,t)\ \ \ \forall a\geqslant 1,t\in[0,T].

Then we have

(6.234)

\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\lambda}(\mathcal{E}^{\delta})\leqslant C(1+\kappa(\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}))\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\delta^{\alpha-2\chi}.

Proof.

Unlike for Lemma
6.31
, we will not give details for all four terms defining the norm
\Theta
. For the sake of
conciseness, we just focus here on estimating

(6.235)

\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{E}^{\delta})\equiv\sup\big\{\mathcal{E}_{r}^{\delta};\,r\in\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket,x\in\llbracket-a,a\rrbracket\big\},

which is a part of
\llbracket\mathcal{E}^{\delta}\rrbracket^{\llbracket 0,t\rrbracket\times\llbracket-a,a\rrbracket}
in (
3.27
). Details are left
to the reader for the other terms.

Let us first give an estimate for the term
\mathcal{E}_{t}^{1,\delta}
in
(
6.196
). To this aim, we write the discrete integral in the right hand side of (
6.196
) as continuous one:

\mathcal{E}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)=-\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}^{\delta}_{t}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\int_{x}^{\lfloor y\rfloor}\tilde{v}_{0}^{\delta}(w)d\hat{U}^{\delta}(w)dy.

Thanks to this expression and the
\hat{U}^{\delta}
-decomposition (
5.4
), one can perform the same kind of analysis as in Section
6.4.1
(for the
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
part) and Proposition
6.14
(for the
\hat{U}_{2}^{\delta}
). This yields

(6.236)

\big|\mathcal{E}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant C(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})+\sqrt{\delta})E(a,t)\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})\cdot\delta^{2}.

Next, we handle the term
\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2,\delta}
in (
6.197
). Again the
\hat{U}_{2}^{\delta}
-part is (trivially) controlled by

C\sqrt{\delta}E(a,t)\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta}).

We therefore focus on the
\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}
-part and further decompose it as

\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)+\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)
, where

(6.237)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t\wedge\delta^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)\left(\int_{x-y}^{\lfloor x-y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)\right)dyds

(6.238)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{t\wedge\delta^{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor x-y\rfloor)\left(\int_{y}^{\lfloor y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)\right)dyds.

We now upper bound the two terms
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)
and
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)
.
The term
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)
is handled as follows: according to (
2.81
) or (
6.80
) we have

(6.239)

\big|\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{0}^{\delta}(\lfloor y\rfloor)\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\delta^{3}}\mathds{1}_{\{y:|y|\leqslant\delta\}}.

In addition the rough integral estimate (
4.5
), properly extended to a discrete setting as in the proof of Lemma
6.17
, entails that

(6.240)

\left|\int_{x-y}^{\lfloor x-y\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)\right|\leqslant C\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E(a,t)e^{-(\lambda+\theta(a+|y|))s}\cdot\left((a+|y|)^{\chi}\delta^{\alpha}\right.\\
\left.+(a+|y|)^{2\chi}\delta^{2\alpha}+(a+|y|)^{2\chi+\beta/2}(\delta^{2\alpha+\beta}+\delta^{\alpha+2\beta})+(a+|y|)^{2\chi}(t-s)^{-\beta/2}\delta^{\alpha+2\beta}\right).

By substituting (
6.239
) and (
6.240
) into the
expression (
6.237
) of
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x),
we obtain that

(6.241)

\big|\mathcal{K}_{t}^{1,\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant C\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Theta(\tilde{v}^{\delta})\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E(a,t)\cdot\delta^{\alpha-2\chi}.

To estimate
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)
, we assume that
t>\delta^{2}

(for otherwise
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)=0
). We first apply
the change of variable
x-y=\sqrt{s}w
to write it as

(6.242)

\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)\triangleq-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\delta^{2}}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sqrt{s}\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{s}w\rfloor)\left(\int_{x-\sqrt{s}w}^{\lfloor x-\sqrt{s}w\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)\right)dwds.

Next recall from (
6.59
) that

(6.243)

\big|\nabla_{x}^{2,\delta}\hat{p}_{\lfloor s\rfloor}^{\delta}(\lfloor\sqrt{s}w\rfloor)\big|\leqslant\frac{C_{1}}{s^{3/2}}e^{-C_{2}w^{2}},\ \ \ \forall s\geqslant\delta^{2},w\in\mathbb{R}.

In addition, invoking again a discrete version of the rough integral estimate (
4.5
), we have

(6.244)

\Big|\int_{x-\sqrt{s}w}^{\lfloor x-\sqrt{s}w\rfloor+\delta}\tilde{v}_{t-s}^{\delta}(z)d\hat{U}_{1}^{\delta}(z)\Big|\leqslant C\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E^{\theta^{\prime}}(a,t)e^{-\left(\lambda+\theta^{\prime}(a+\sqrt{T}|w|)\right)s}\\
\times\Big((a+\sqrt{T}|w|)^{\chi}\delta^{\alpha}+(a+\sqrt{T}|w|)^{2\chi}\delta^{2\alpha}+(a+\sqrt{T}|w|)^{2\chi+\beta/2}(\delta^{2\alpha+\beta}+\delta^{\alpha+2\beta})\\
+\big(a+\sqrt{T}|w|\big)^{2\chi}(t-s)^{-\beta/2}\delta^{\alpha+2\beta}\Big),

where we recall that
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
and
\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}
denotes a uniform
upper bound of
\Theta^{\theta^{\prime},\lambda}(v^{\delta})
. As before,
the role of
\theta^{\prime}
is to absorb the extra polynomial factors in

a
, i.e. we choose
\theta^{\prime}<\theta
so that (
6.233
) holds. By substituting (
6.243
) and (
6.244
) into the
expression (
6.238
) of
\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x),
there are four individual
terms coming out (corresponding to the four terms in (
6.244
)).
The first three terms lead to an
s
-integral

\int_{\delta^{2}}^{t}\frac{1}{s}ds=\log\frac{t}{\delta^{2}}=O(\log\delta).

The last term leads to an
s
-integral

\int_{\delta^{2}}^{t}s^{-1}(t-s)^{-\beta/2}ds=t^{-\beta/2}\int_{\delta^{2}/t}^{1}\rho^{-1}(1-\rho)^{-\beta/2}d\rho\leqslant\delta^{-\beta}O(\log\delta).

To summarize, we obtain that

(6.245)

\big|\mathcal{K}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant C\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E^{\theta}(a,t)\delta^{\alpha}\log\delta.

Combining the above estimates (also noting that
\alpha-2\chi<\alpha<1/2
and
\Lambda^{\theta,\beta}\leqslant\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}
), we can now conclude our
\mathcal{E}^{2,\delta}
-estimate as

(6.246)

\big|\mathcal{E}_{t}^{2,\delta}(x)\big|\leqslant C(\kappa(\hat{{\bf U}}_{1}^{\delta})+1)\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}E^{\theta}(a,t)\cdot\delta^{\alpha-2\chi}.

Eventually putting together (
6.236
) and
(
6.246
) into the decomposition (
6.232
) we obtain the desired estimate (
6.234
).
∎

We finish this section by proving our main result.

Proof of Proposition
6.28
.

Gathering our estimates in Sections
6.5.2
to
6.5.4
the proof of relation (
6.194
) is achieved.
∎

6.6.
Completing the proof of Proposition
6.5

We now put together all estimates obtained so far to establish our main estimate (
6.21
). To ease notation, we will write

\kappa_{\delta}\triangleq\kappa_{\alpha,\chi}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\delta}),\kappa\triangleq\kappa_{\alpha,\chi}(\mathbf{W}),\rho_{\delta}\triangleq\rho_{\alpha,\chi}(\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\delta},\mathbf{W}),\Theta\triangleq\Theta^{\theta,\lambda}(v),\Theta_{\delta}\triangleq\Theta^{\theta,\lambda}(\tilde{v}^{\delta}),

and also

d(\cdot,\cdot)\triangleq d_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\delta},\mathbf{W}}(\cdot,\cdot).

In addition,
C_{i}
will denote constants depending on all underlying
exponents but not on
\delta,\lambda
. With all exponents
\alpha,\beta,\chi,\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}

given fixed, it follows from Lemmas
6.16
,
6.17
,
6.23
,
6.24
,
6.25
and Proposition
6.28
that

\displaystyle d(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)

\displaystyle\leqslant d(\mathcal{W}^{1,\delta},\mathcal{W})+\Theta_{\hat{\mathbf{U}}_{1}^{\delta}}^{\theta,\lambda}(\mathcal{W}^{2,\delta})+d(\mathcal{J}^{\delta},\eta)

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{1}\left[\lambda^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}\kappa d(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)+(\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}})\delta^{\beta}\right.

(6.247)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \left.+\lambda^{\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}(1+\kappa_{\delta}+\kappa)(\Theta_{\delta}+\Theta+\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}})(\rho_{\delta}+\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}})\right].

We now fix a choice of
\lambda=\lambda_{\omega}
which satisfies

\lambda^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta^{\prime}}{4}}\bar{\kappa}(\omega)\leqslant\frac{1}{4}

so that (cf. Lemma
6.15
and Lemma
6.22
)

\sup_{\delta\in(0,1]}\Theta_{\delta}\vee\Theta\vee\Lambda^{\theta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime}}\leqslant C_{2}e^{C_{3}\lambda}\left(\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}\right)\bar{\kappa}(\omega),

where we recall that
\bar{\kappa}(\omega)
is defined by (
6.19
).
It follows from (
6.247
) that

(6.248)

\displaystyle d(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)

\displaystyle\leqslant\lambda^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}C_{1}\kappa d(\tilde{v}^{\delta},v)+C_{4}e^{C_{5}\lambda}\left(\|f_{0}\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}+\|g\|_{\mathcal{C}_{L}^{3}}\right)\left(\delta^{\beta}+(1+\bar{\kappa}(\omega))^{2}(\rho_{\delta}+\delta^{\frac{\beta^{\prime}(\beta^{\prime}-\beta)}{\beta^{\prime}+\beta}})\right).

By enlarging
\lambda
if necessary to ensure that
\lambda^{-\frac{\alpha-\beta}{4}}C_{1}\kappa\leqslant 1/2
,
one can then move the first term on the right hand side of (
6.248
)
to the left and the desired estimate (
6.21
) thus follows.
